B.13.1 INTRODUCTION
The execution of the (Project Name) Scheme across multiple sites and by a
number of CONTRACTORS requires that information supplied is consistent and
complete.
During the execution of the WORK, COMPANY will station COMPANY PROJECT
Team personnel (including PROJECT MANAGEMENT CONSULTANT, PMC
personnel) in CONTRACTOR’S home office(s) and SITES. COMPANY may also
visit and be stationed at SUBCONTRACTOR’S offices and manufacturer’s
establishments. The requirements stated in this EXHIBIT for CONTRACTOR’S
home office shall apply to all offices that CONTACTOR may use during execution.
References to COMPANY herein also include PROJECT MANAGEMENT
CONSULTANT /PMC personnel.
B.13.1.1 Company Expectations
CONTRACTOR is required to provide project solutions to address COMPANY’S
primary concerns in the areas of document management and control and data
management as follows:
Exchange of data between participants
The timely exchange of information and data must permit all PARTIES to proceed
with their respective scope of work without delays, the exchange of information
must be:
▪ Timely
▪ Correctly transmitted
▪ The correct information
Consistency of data between participants:
To ensure consistency attention must be paid to:
▪ Data formats
▪ Data requirements
▪ Data turnovers
▪ Document formats
▪ Electronic media content/formats
▪ Film media content/format
▪ Method of Document/data turnover to COMPANY
Long term storage and maintenance of PROJECT data
As the PROJECT nears completion and CONTRACTOR turns the facility
documentation over to COMPANY, the responsibility for storage, maintenance,
and availability of the documentation will shift from CONTRACTOR to
COMPANY.

It is critical at this point in time that the required systems necessary to store,
maintain and provide ready access to the facility documentation and data is
operational. The systems and data must be available to the operators and other
end users.
During the PROJECT life cycle it will be necessary to organize the vast quantity of
information in a manner such that it can be readily retrieved for reference or
modification as required. CONTRACTOR should satisfy COMPANY requirement of
handling over final document / data to operators in efficient, usable format at time of
handover.
The information and software applications will adhere to the agreed and
established COMPANY standards and allow for future maintainability of the
data/information.
Pre-planning for intelligent plant functionality
COMPANY is committed to the development of advanced systems that integrate
many different aspects of plant operations and maintenance. The building and
integration of the various aspects and functionality of these systems during the
design phase must be pre-planned in order to ensure a proper level of integration is
achieved to COMPANY’S satisfaction and the value added potential of this effort is
maximized.
The Information provided by CONTRACTOR may be required in specific forms to
ensure that Plant operations needs are satisfied.
B.13.1.2 Contractor Responsibilities
CONTRACTOR shall use industry latest best practices to ensure business
continuity in all WORK locations. The IT infrastructure must be sufficiently sized to
cope with the demands of the PROJECT. COMPANY shall request upgrades
where connections and systems are impacting on engineering progress.
CONTRACTOR shall provide suitably qualified and responsible individuals to whom
COMPANY can communicate with respect to PROJECT automation and it
infrastructure.
As a hold point (witness point) contractor shall obtain COMPANY’s IT approval and
acceptance:
• For all materials in EPC phase.
• For all contractor’s proposals in design phase.
• For the all points checked in the checked list (Matrix Check list).
• For every signed general statement by the contractor in every project phase.
• For every IT systems FAT, SAT, ISAT, Handover phases were IT focal points
should attend every FAT, SAT, ISAT and Handover phases and sign.
• For all IT infrastructures as built drawings submitted by contractor.

B.13.1.3 Networks
CONTRACTOR shall establish on the PROJECT an environment that supports
electronic transmittal of data and information to the maximum extent possible.
CONTRACTOR shall provide and use a network that shall interconnect all
participants, major VENDORS, the SITE, and other companies or offices
COMPANY deems necessary to adequately support the PROJECT.
CONTRACTOR shall provide all information security measures to ensure protection
of PROJECT for COMPANY in this Automation Plan.
B.13.1.4 Video Conferencing
COMPANY views video conferencing as a valuable tool in the performance of the
PROJECT that can provide significant savings in terms of reduced travel costs and
problem resolution. CONTRACTOR shall provide the basic telecommunications
network capability permitting attachment of their internal teleconferencing
equipment to other participants and/or COMPANY.
B.13.1.5 Document / Data Management and Control
To avoid document and data turnover problems COMPANY has adopted standards
that the CONTRACTOR shall be compliant with.
An Electronic Document Management System (EDMS) shall be used by the
CONTRACTOR to store, control and distribute PROJECT documentation.
CONTRACTOR EDMS system shall be used by CONTRACTOR and PMC/PMT at
both COMPANY and PMC Home offices and SITE office.
The CONTRACTOR upon award will ensure timely availability of PROJECT
documentation for Company’s staff in HQ and SITE office. CONTRACTOR upon
award will formally request names and other information required to set up
COMPANY (including PMT/PMC) personnel, early access to CONTRACTOR
EDMS by COMPANY is of the highest priority after award.
The document distribution matrix is to be approved by COMPANY.
B.13.1.6 Date Centers:
Company requires contractor to follow all COMPANY’s standards and
specifications in Data Centers Design and Implementation. All current and future
requirements of the project and the COMPANY should be considered in the DC.
COMPANY DC are tier 3 or tier 4, typical requirement as below should be
considered:
- Location, Size and Access:
o Consideration for future expansion, Appropriate
o Proximity from EMI Sources
o Size as per current and future requirement
o Access to be granted to Authorized personnel with Door Access Controllers.
- Exterior and Interior of the Data Centre:
o The building should have at least 2 entry/exit doors

B.13.1.7 Engineering, Material & Maintenance Data Integration
COMPANY requires CONTRACTOR to take into account plant operational

B.13.1.8 Telecoms:
Contractor has to follow Company standards in implementation of the Telecom
infrastructure requirement of the project. All cables, patch panels, equipment and
accessories, should be from Company approved vendor list and should be installed

application for project.

• SmartPlant Foundation (SPF)

requirements for the EPC project.

System and Integrator for SPE tools to Publish and retrieve data.
This document describes SPF specification for this project.

1.6 SMART PLANT FOUNDATION /SMARTPLANT ENTERPRISE
SmartPlant Foundation (SPF) is a single data source, ensuring data accuracy, integrity,
and security; improving accessibility and control of work processes; and making plant
data readily available to everyone in the enterprise and beyond. All plant/project
information is consolidated into one consistent, managed data repository. SmartPlant
Enterprise (SPE) integration allows you to standardize data and improve communication
among the authoring tools.
SmartPlant Enterprise full integration provides the following features:
• Transfer of engineering data from one tool to another.
• Management of change resulting from ongoing engineering in upstream
applications.
• Accessibility of engineering information to other collaborators without requiring the
original engineering tools.

1.7 PLANT BREAKDOWN STRUCTURE
1.7.1 PBS Hierarchy and SPE System Integration
A Plant Breakdown Structure (PBS) is a classified hierarchy with superiors, or roots, and
subordinates, or dependents. For example, a PBS for organizing plant data might be
plant/area/unit.
In SmartPlant Foundation and the authoring tools that are integrated with it, engineering
data is organized using a plant breakdown structure (PBS).
PBS is published from SPF and retrieved by SPE Authoring tools (SPPID, SPEL, SP3D
and SPI) to correlate with tool PBS.
Plant
A plant is a logical unit of data that is normally used to model a single physical plant. SPF
is plant based and its classification is available OOTB.
Functional Area
This is a core object, referred to as Functional Area and is available OOTB. This structure
provides better categorization of data.
Functional Unit
Group of parts of the schematic and individual worlds of a plant that together perform a
given process function. The identifying number of the unit is unique within the project and
within the plant. Most companies, but not all, use the concept of unit.
1.7.2 Integration Hierarchy Limitations
The integrated environment imposes the following limitations on custom hierarchies, based
on requirements from the authoring tools. This list includes the tool imposing each
restriction, so that customers can factor these limitations into their hierarchy setup based
on their current and future tool usage.
1. The hierarchy must have a minimum of 3 levels. This limitation is imposed by
SmartPlant Instrumentation.
2. The names of hierarchy items cannot be changed once created. This limitation is
imposed by SmartPlant Foundation.
3. The hierarchy scheme cannot be modified after the plant is created. This limitation is
imposed by SmartPlant P&ID and SmartPlant Foundation.
4. In PBS, Names of Area, Unit should match with the one present in SPF and Authoring
Tools (SPPID, SPEL, SP3D and SPI)

1.9  SCHEMA MAPPING
SmartPlant integration is based on a publish/retrieve paradigm. Applications publish their
own data and retrieve data published by other applications. Publish and retrieve processes
are done by Mapping (in Schema) reference data among the different tools.
1.9.1  Schema Modelling Workflow
CONTRACTOR  shall  follow  below  workflow  for  creating/editing  reference  data  and
creating/editing SmartPlant schema. CONTRACTOR shall validate the schema before it is
loaded in the SPF Database.
Standard practice involves the steps below:
1.  Add custom attributes in Tool databases.
2.  Integrate SPF with Application tools
3.  Check out CMF in the SPF Desktop Client and Launch Schema Editor
4.  Synchronize tool map schema and tool metadata
5.  Map objects or attributes if they already exists in SmartPlant schema

7. Map those tool schema objects into (publish) and out of (retrieve) the SmartPlant

8. Validate Tool Map Schema and SmartPlant schema.
9. Resolve any inconsistencies in schemas.
10. Export the customizations done in CMF

materials class with temperatures, pressures, and diameters assigned to the pipe
run and to search commodity codes for piping components.

1.10 REPORTS CUSTOMIZATION
CONTRACTOR shall generate required reports from respective application tools such as
SPPID, SPI, SPEL and SP3D. CONTRACTOR shall configure these reports in SPF based
on COMPANY requirements.
Reports can be run on an ad-hoc basis where the user can enter search criteria each time
as required. Ad hoc Reports are based on view definitions which are in turn based on
graph definitions. The graph definition is a collection of edge definitions that represent a
path through the data model.
Once the system administrator has set up the necessary definitions, the user can login to
the Desktop client to generate a report. The available properties to be reported on cannot
be changed; but, the user can choose a subset of the properties, from the definition types,
to be included in each iteration of a report generated.
1.11 INCONSISTENCY REPORT
SmartPlant Foundation has tools to review data inconsistencies and to view data as
published by each authoring tool within SmartPlant Foundation Desktop Client.
The separation of published information by application into published domains, coupled
with the correlation of like objects saved within shared domain, provides the ability to
generate inconsistency report.
CONTRACTOR shall configure Excel reports (Inconsistency reports) to be run on
SPFTEFSharedObjects.
CONTRACTOR shall configure proper access so that users can select a single shared
object or multiple shared objects in desktop client list view and all this report from the right-
click menu. The report is viewed in Excel, and the reports highlight the inconsistent
properties.

Overview of Backup SmartPlant Foundation Server
Ensuring data integrity on the SmartPlant Foundation server requires backing up several
key components on the server:
• SmartPlant Foundation Server Software
Page 42 of 214

ADNOC Classification: Internal
EPC WORK FOR RUWAIS SULPHUR GRANULATION
PLANT AT RSHT-2 FOR HAIL & GHASHA PROJECT
PROJECT NO:
AGREEMENT NO: 4700025948 1005312
INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY (IT)
ADNOC GAS EXHIBIT - B.13 REV. 1
The SmartPlant Foundation application software needs to be backed up only when
any custom API is built or any changes are made to delivered API’s, or after a
modification to a custom icon or custom report.
• SmartPlant Foundation Configuration - The SmartPlant Foundation configuration
data, which is stored in the SmartPlant Foundation data database schema.
The SmartPlant Foundation configuration is stored in a relational database schema
in Oracle. This data needs to be backed up on a daily basis. This backup is
essential for the protection and recovery of SmartPlant Foundation installation.
• SmartPlant Foundation File Data
SmartPlant Foundation files are stored in disk areas logically grouped into vaults,
as specified in the SmartPlant Foundation configuration. Every file transaction
causes changes to the relevant vault, daily backups are recommended to prevent
any data loss.
• SmartPlant Schema Files
Back up the delivered EFSchema.cmf file and any customized versions of the
schema files.
• Templates – Such as, Excel templates, report templates, or data sheet templates.
• IIS Configuration - Virtual directories, application pools, security settings, and so
forth.

COMPANY’s SPF with COMPANY’s IT Engineer according to COMPANY’s IT policy.
COMPANY’s representative should be present during SAT. Contractor will setup authoring
tools at site, register authoring tools to COMPANY’s SPF, create necessary mapping
configuration and publish or load the necessary data. COMPANY should fix the
independent SAT execution date with Contractor at least one month before its execution.
COMPANY’s representative should be present during SAT. Company will check and
accept Contractor’s data and system environments.
Contractor will prepare integrated SAT procedure which contains integrated SAT execution
procedure, schedule, scope and responsibility of each party and acceptance criteria for
COMPANY. Contractor will execute integrated SAT at site. COMPANY’s representative
should be present during integrated SAT. Consistency check and validation of all party’s
authoring tools and data will be carried out. If COMPANY has comments, COMPANY will
list up the comments and each party should take responsibility to clear their comments.
After integrated SAT, COMPANY checks and accepts the final SPF.
IT senior Smart Plant specialist representative presence is a must to attend FAT & SAT
related to Smart plant foundation and Smart plant tools for any project, in order to make
sure that the final SP system handover is as per COMPANY requirements from all aspects
including hardware, software, backup, configuration, scalability and performance.
1.19 INTELLECTUAL PROPERTY RIGHTS (IPR)
The Intellectual Property Rights of all COMPANY’s Custom Applications/Modules shall be
considered proprietary to COMPANY. These customizations is not to be shared with
individuals/companies outside of Consultant’s organization unless COMPANY has
specifically provided either written consent allowing said individuals/companies access to
this information. Except ADNOC Group of companies who can reuse all COMPANY’s
Custom Applications/Modules, however implementation/training charges will be at the cost
of each company depending on scope of work.

1.37.1.1 SPPID Implementation Plan
SPPID Implementation Plan shall cover all aspects of SPPID setup and customization
carried out during the EPC phase. CONTRACTOR shall prepare and submit this document
for COMPANY approval at the start of EPC phase.
The minimum contents shall be:
• Roles & Responsibilities of Key SPPID Personnel (Administrators, Lead designers,
users)
• Setup information
• Plant Hierarchy (PBS) structure
• Reference Data implementation plan
• Instructions for Designers for use of correct Symbols, Labels to be used
• Instructions for resolving Inconsistencies in SPPID drawings
• Setup for Reports outputs
• The SP-P&ID Checklist given in ‘Appendix – C’ is to be updated by the CONTRACTOR
during each data handover stage.
• Backup
• Data maintenance procedure (such as Database constraint checks, database
maintenance, Duplicate Items check)
1.37.1.2 SPPID Customization Document
SPPID Customization Document shall keep record and track all SPPID customizations
carried out for during the EPC phase.
The minimum contents shall be:
• A list of all customization that are applied to SPPID on completion of the EPC phase.
• Details must be provided for each customization change and should include the
requirement for change, method of configuration, screenshots of changes made,
COMPANY approval date and comments.
1.37.1.3 SPPID Exceptions and Deviations Document
SPPID Exceptions and Deviations Document shall keep record of all exceptions and
deviations from SPPID Implementation Plan during EPC phase.
The minimum contents shall be:
• Details of deviations and exceptions from SPPID Implementation Plan.
• Details shall include Requirement of Exception/Deviation, Screenshots showing exact
deviations/exception. Date of Approval by COMPANY and Comments by COMPANY.

1.37.2 Engineering Drawing Requirements for P&ID
It is required that guidelines given below shall be followed:
• Border templates and title blocks for the P&IDs shall be customized to match
COMPANY border standards.
• All piping, equipment and instruments and their interconnections shall be shown as per
P&ID Legend sheets developed for Project.
• All applicable data for Pipe run, Equipment and Instrument shall be filled. Please refer
to Sec 1.10 (mandatory attributes) of SPE Tools Implementation for attribute data
population details.
• All lines shall have flow directions clearly shown.
• Equipment description, Tag. No. and design parameters shall appear either near the
top or near the bottom of the sheet directly above or below Equipment symbol.
• The contractor is required to ensure that P&ID instrument tags have association with
the appropriate instrument loops.
• The Layout of P&IDs shall allow for future additions and shall minimize crossing lines.
Vertical line shall be broken when it crosses with Horizontal line. Secondary Lines shall
be broken when crossing Primary line. Utility lines shall be broken when crossing
Process lines. Instrument lines shall be broken when crossing flow lines.
• Piping Specification changes shall be clearly shown.
• Insulation on Piping and Equipment shall be shown. Heat tracing requirements shall
also be indicated.
• Any template / symbol to be used on the project, if not already provided by COMPANY
has to be created by CONTRACTOR. Only on approval by COMPANY has this to be
implemented by CONTRACTOR.
• As far as possible content shown on P&ID shall be intelligent i.e. labels, design values,
properties shown on P&ID shall be present in database. Use of non-intelligent text and
graphics shall be minimized. Notes and Detail arrangements can be shown using non-
intelligent text and graphics.
1.37.3 Vendor Packages
P&ID drawings for Vendor Packages shall be incorporated in SPPID by CONTRACTOR as
per COMPANY requirement.
CONTRACTOR shall confirm exact VENDOR Package Details with COMPANY at the time
of award of EPC contract.
1.37.4 Data Checks and Cleanup
Prior to data handover CONTRACTOR shall ensure that SPPID drawings and data is not
corrupt and do not contain inconsistencies. All SPPID inconsistencies shall be resolved by

2.1 INTRODUCTION
This section describes the basic information about SmartPlant Instrumentation project set
up for new COMPANY-Projects. It includes, the information regarding software and
database versions, database creation, P-A-U hierarchy and permission groups.
For the business aspects of any engineering applications; contractor should refer to
COMPANY TTS department's Engineering Data Deliverable Standard (TT-ST-001) for
more clarifications. If full understanding is achieved then contractor can go further with the
following technical information in this document to achieve the proper implementation.
Engineering Data Deliverable Standard (TT-ST-001)updated version Link:
https://edms.gasco.ae/livelink/livelink.exe/Open/59480692.

2.13 SmartPlant Instrumentation (SPI) CONTRACTOR IMPLEMENTATION
2.13.1 CONTRACTOR Implementation Documents
CONTRACTOR shall prepare following procedures and documents:
1. SPI Implementation Plan
2. SPI Customization Document
3. SPI Exceptions and Deviations Document
These documents shall be based on COMPANY requirements in this document internal
best practices of CONTRACTOR.
2.13.1.1 SmartPlant Instrumentation - Implementation Plan
This document shall cover all aspects of SPI setup, done for the project. CONTRACTOR
shall prepare and submit this document for COMPANY approval at the start of EPC phase.
The contents must include (as a minimum) the following:
• Roles & Responsibilities of key SPI Personnel (Administrators, Lead Designers and
Users)
• Set-up Information
• Plant Hierarchy (PBS) Structure.
• Reference Data implementation plan.
• List of attributes to be populated.
• List of User Defined fields, User Defined Tables, Custom Spec. Forms, Custom Title
Blocks, Instrument Type Profiles, Reference Cables / Panels, ESL Symbols and
Templates for Loops and Hook-up’s Report outputs.
• Report formats for Instrument Index, I/O list, Cable schedule, Loop Drawings, Hook-Up
drawings and others as specified by COMPANY.
• The SPI Checklist given in ‘Appendix – B’ is to be updated by the CONTRACTOR
during each data handover stage.
• Backup .
• Data maintenance procedure (such as integrity checks, database maintenance, etc.).
2.13.1.2 SPI Customization Document
SPI Customization Document shall keep record, and track all SPI customizations carried
out during the EPC phase.
The minimum contents shall be:
• A list of all customizations that is applicable to SPI, on completion of the EPC phase.
• Details must be provided for each customization change and should include the
requirement for change, method of configuration, screenshots of changes made,
COMPANY approval date and comments

2.13.1.4 Engineering Drawing Requirements for Instrumentation
It is required that guidelines given below shall be followed:
• Instrument Index, I/O List, Specification (Data Sheet) Sheet report formats and other
such reports, as desired by COMPANY, shall be customized to match COMPANY
standards.
• Title blocks for outputs from the various SPI modules ( viz. Instrument Index, Wiring
Module etc.. ) shall be customized to match COMPANY standards.
• Border templates and title blocks for Instrument Loop Drawings and Hook-Up Drawings
shall be customized to match COMPANY standards.
• Enhanced Smart Report symbols, for Loop / Hook-Up drawings shall be customized to
match COMPANY standards.
• All applicable data for plant items (viz. Instrument, Panel, Cable etc.. shall be filled.
Please refer to Sec 1.11 (mandatory attributes) for attribute data population details.
• The contractor is required to ensure that the relevant instrument tags have association
with the appropriate instrument loops.
• Any template / symbol to be used on the project, if not already provided by COMPANY
have to be created by CONTRACTOR. Only on approval by COMPANY has this to be
implemented by CONTRACTOR.
• As far as possible content shown on Enhanced Smart Report drawings ( Loop
Drawings / Hook-Up drawings ) shall be intelligent i.e. labels, design value and
properties shown. Use of non-intelligent text and graphics shall be minimized.

3.1 INTRODUCTION
This section describes the basic information about SmartPlant Electrical project set up for
new COMPANY-Projects. It includes, the information regarding software and database
versions, database creation, P-A-U hierarchy and permission groups.
3.2 SOFTWARE VERSIONS
Engineering Tools Database
SmartPlant Electrical latest version As compatible with application
The various software used and their versions are detailed below.
SPEL Version:
Oracle Version:
Database character set: AL32UTF8
NLS character set: AL16UTF16.
Note:
• CONTRACTOR shall use latest Application version. Final handover to be done in the
version complying with that of COMPANY.
• SPEL version shall be compatible with versions of all other SmartPlant tools used in the
project i.e. SP-P&ID, SPI, SP3D and SPF.
• CONTRACTOR shall configure SPEL in an integrated environment with SPF
• After the EPC and As-Built updating is completed, CONTRACTOR shall upgrade the
SPEL data to the version, as specified and approved by COMPANY. CONTRACTOR
shall handover upgraded SPEL data to COMPANY.
• The SPEL version upgrade is considered part of the EPC scope of work and hence
COMPANY does not accept project delays arising from the SPEL upgrade.

SmartPlant Electrical shall be implemented on a version, approved by the COMPANY using
the compatible version of Oracle, also confirmed by COMPANY.
Intergraph recommends separate database instance for SmartPlant Electrical. viz. Oracle
instance ‘SPEL’. The procedure for database instance creation has been described, in the
‘SmartPlant Electrical Installation Guide’. SPEL project related data will reside in this oracle
Instance.

Schedule and others as specified by COMPANY.

during each data handover stage.

carried out during the EPC phase.

requirement for change, method of configuration, screenshots of changes made,
COMPANY approval date and comments

3.14.1.4 Engineering Drawing Requirements for Electrical
It is required that guidelines given below shall be followed:
• Border templates and title blocks for the Drawings / Documents shall be customized to
match COMPANY standards.
• All SLD symbols used in SLD generation shall be shown as per the electrical legend
sheets developed for Project.
• All Schematic symbols used in Schematic generation shall be shown to confirm to
project specifications.
• All applicable data for plant items (viz. Motor, Cable, Generator etc.. shall be filled.
Please refer to Sec 1.12 (mandatory attributes) for attribute data population details.
• The contractor is required to ensure that the relevant instrument tags have association
with the appropriate instrument loops.
• Any template / symbol to be used on the project, if not already provided by COMPANY
have to be created by CONTRACTOR. Only on approval by COMPANY has this to be
implemented by CONTRACTOR.
• As far as possible content shown on SLD / Schematic drawings shall be intelligent i.e.
labels, design value and properties shown. Use of non-intelligent text and graphics shall
be minimized.
• SLD’s generated from SPEL shall be annotated by CONTRACTOR as required to have
proper presentation.

SLD’s, Schematic drawings from Vendor Packages shall be incorporated in SPEL by
CONTRACTOR as per COMPANY requirement.
CONTRACTOR shall confirm exact VENDOR Package Details with COMPANY at the time
of award of EPC contract.

3.14.2 Reference Data Management
Reference Data includes all items viz. Ref. Profiles, Ref Electrical Items, Ref Cables, Look-
Up Tables, Ref. Typical Blocks, Ref. Typical Schematic, Ref. Typical Cable Block
Diagrams, Ref Typical I/O sets, Ref Terminal Strip Configurations etc..
All changes (additions, modifications and deletions) of SPEL Reference Data during EPC
phase shall be managed and tracked using procedure described in this section.
a. A log file is to be used for recording and tracking all SPEL reference data change
requests. The format of the log file is included in Appendix-A.
b. If existing SPEL reference data is inadequate for engineering, during EPC phase,
CONTRACTOR SPEL application Administrator shall send a change request to
COMPANY Application Specialist. Supporting screenshots, symbols or files shall be
provided as required. All change requests should be submitted using the change
request log file.
c. COMPANY Application Specialist shall review the request and provide approval or
alternative feedback. COMPANY engineering specialists (e.g. process, piping,
instrumentation or electrical engineers) will be consulted for approval where required.
Consultation shall be recorded and updated in change request log file.
d. CONTRACTOR SPEL Administrator (as directed by the COMPANY Application
Specialist) shall update SPEL application reference data to reflect approved change
requests only.
e. CONTRACTOR is required to modify reference / plant data through Reference data
change management Procedure.

4.4.2 Naming Rules
CONTRACTOR shall use COMPANY naming convention for equipment, nozzles,
pipelines, columns, beams, etc. Naming convention shall use the full tag number and be
identical to that in SPF/SPPID/SPI/SPEL.
All tagging shall be done using naming rules. Naming rules shall be created in SP3D using
VB program which generates the names for each object.
The naming convention followed for all the tags such as line number, equipment number,
instrument number shall conform to
• COMPANY reference documents.
• Integration guidelines.
4.4.3 Attributes
CONTRACTOR shall populate the attributes to produce required documents, such as
isometrics, plan and elevation drawings, piping material takeoffs and other reports.
CONTRACTOR shall maintain consistency of SP3D attributes with SPPID, SPI & SPEL &
SPF.
Creation of any new attributes shall be done after approval from COMPANY.
4.4.4 Piping
The minimum content of piping discipline shall be as listed below:
1. All major process attributes and identification attributes shall be captured. Attributes
shall be retrieved from SPF/SPP&ID/SPI using the integration facility to maintain
consistency.
2. All applicable data for Pipe run shall be filled. As a minimum, Normal Operating
Pressure & Temperature, Normal Design Pressure & Temperature, specification,
insulation information etc.
3. Fabrication and construction properties such as PWHT, X-RAY, test pressure shall be
populated.
4. Above ground and underground piping.
5. All process and utility piping including all piping components.
6. All valves indicated in the P&ID.
7. Valves including hand wheels, actuators, chain operators, motor operators, gear
operators, etc.
8. Pipe supports (shall be modeled as physical support. Logical supports are not
acceptable).
9. Jacketed & core pipes wherever applicable.

4.4.16 Data Checks & Cleanup
CONTRACTOR shall perform regular maintenance of the database for optimal
performance as per guidelines mentioned in SP3D documents. The model shall be as
“clean” as possible without any integrity errors and TO DO LIST entries.

DO LIST entries are not in control.
As a minimum, the following checks shall be performed:
1. TO-DO-LIST entries shall be monitored and cleared on a daily basis.
2. Daily backup of the plant, shared content folder and customized files.
3. Interferences shall be resolved on a regular basis.
4. Database Integrity checks shall be performed regularly and all inconsistencies shall be
resolved.
5. Database maintenance shall be performed.

Reference data. These include view styles, drawing templates, rules for dimensions, rules
for labels etc.
CONTRACTOR shall customize SP3D catalog to include all sizes for all piping components
such that it matches EPC phase piping material specifications.
All customizations shall be recorded in a document (excel file) and submitted to COMPANY
for approval. These records shall be included in SmartPlant 3D Customization Document.
CONTRACTOR shall backup all bulkload excel files, source codes for symbols, rules,
support assemblies etc. for handover to COMPANY.
4.7 Backup of Project Data
An established backup plan is mandatory for SP3D database as a means of disaster
recovery. The right backup strategy depends on your business requirements and activity
on the database.
CONTRACTOR shall implement a robust procedure for backup and include this in
‘SmartPlant 3D Implementation Procedure’. As a minimum, the plants shall be backed up
on a daily basis. SP3D tools and utilities provide facilities for scheduling the backup
command.
4.8 Hardware, software & licenses for COMPANY
CONTRACTOR shall provide the required hardware, software & licenses at COMPANY
office for review purposes. These shall be purchased and licensed in COMPANY’s name
and maintained by CONTRACTOR for the project duration.

B.13.9 ELECTRONIC DOCUMENT MANAGEMENT SYSTEM
From EFFECTIVE DATE until FINAL ACCEPTANCE, the CONTRACTOR shall
operate a fully functional EDMS which is configured to operate in the Oil and Gas
Industry. This system shall be supported 24/7/365, Web enabled and allow
acceptable response times for standard user requirements.
The CONTRACTOR will obtain the latest COMPANY LiveLink EPC Deliverables
Loader ‘CONTRACTORS GUIDE’ upon AWARD. An example is shown in
Appendix-C.
B.13.9.1 Access to System
The information contained in the EDMS shall be easily and reliably available to both
CONTRACTOR and COMPANY at all locations where WORK is being performed.
B.13.9.2 Availability and Distribution
From the EFFECTIVE DATE until FINAL ACCEPTANCE, CONTRACTOR shall
implement and maintain an Electronic Document Management System (EDMS) for
the PROJECT. The EDMS electronically store all deliverables together with
metadata requirements defined in EXHIBIT B.42. The system architecture shall be
based on an industry standard, non-proprietary, commercially available software
application and hardware components. The application shall meet the requirements
listed in this section
B.13.9.3 Training
CONTRACTOR shall provide COMPANY personnel located in CONTRACTOR
offices adequate training in the operation of their EDMS system. Training shall be
provided at no added cost to the COMPANY, therefore included in the LUMP SUM
PRICE. Training will cover COMPANY personnel in Abu Dhabi, HQ,PMC and at
SITE.

B.13.13 PIMS REQUIREMENTS.
B.13.14 INTRODUCTION
The primary purpose of the pipeline construction data submission specification is to
support the exchange of the pipelines construction data between the Contractor
and the Company. This requirement stemmed out of the Company’s initiative in
consolidating all the pipelines construction data into one central database.
Accordingly Company developed the central database based on the model named
ArcGIS Pipeline Data Model (APDM). The central database is evolving with
different kinds of data related to pipelines. The central database currently can host:
Survey Data, Construction Data, Piping & Fittings Data, Inline Inspection Data, and
Field Inspections however can be customized to store any kind of pipelines related
data.
Since the Contractors submit the pipelines construction data in different electronic
documents such as: Alignment Sheets, Design Basis, Data Sheets, Plot plans,
General Arrangement and Piping Layout, and Isometrics, manually loading data
from all these scattered sources is slow, erroneous, and time consuming process.
In order to overcome these challenges, Company has developed a standard
template for pipelines construction data submission for the Contractor and this
document explains the specifications of that standard template.
Some of the benefits of adopting the standard include fast, error free, and smooth
data migration into the Company’s central database which ultimately help in the
long-term improvement of the pipelines network.
B.13.14.1 Scope, Purpose, and Application
B.13.14.1.1 Scope
The pipelines encompass a wide range of spatial data including many elements
critical to the public and private infrastructure. The reliable functioning of the
increasingly complex, inter-connected, and inter-dependent gas transmission is vital
for the security, economic prosperity, and social well-being of the Emirate. The
standard defines a Geospatial Data Submission (GDS) Standard for surveying,
engineering, and planning data submitted by contractors, consultants, and
developers to the Company.
B.13.14.1.2 Purpose
This document provides a framework for the Company and the Contractor to
streamline the pipeline construction data exchange process. Consequently, this
standard has two purposes:
▪ Enabling the Company to accommodate the requirements for geospatial data
submittals in terms of specific geospatial features and attributes
▪ Enabling contractors to standardize their work processes for geospatial data
submittals, saving time and money.

B.13.14.1.3 Application
These standards apply to all surveying, planning, and construction data submitted
by contractors, consultants, and developers. The implementation of this standards
framework allows GIS and CAD data to be integrated into the Company’s central
database. This document is intended to facilitate a common methodology to
manage and share pipelines datasets. The pipelines transmit different products
from generating and processing plants to the industrial sites and substations using
transmission networks. Transmission pipelines contains the following data,
▪ Design and basic pipeline data
▪ Manufacturing (mill records), pipeline material and installation (incl. hydrostatic
testing) data
▪ Geodetic pipeline geometry and route data
▪ Resistivity
▪ Pipeline components and associated data
▪ Historical product data (incl. chemical composition)
▪ Internal inspection (intelligent pigging) data
▪ External inspection data
▪ Third Party Activity
▪ Development around the corridor.
The non-network structural support features located along the network are also part
of this data set. These features when combined with other utility features offer a
high-level appreciation of the degree of infrastructure interdependency.
B.13.14.2 ArcGIS Pipeline Data Model (APDM)
B.13.14.2.1 Overview
The pipeline network is used to route natural gas to several clients, such as Abu
Dhabi Water and Electricity Company (ADWEC), Industrial City of Abu Dhabi
(ICAD), Emirates Aluminium Company (EMAL), Emirates Steel Industries (ESI),
MASDAR and many more.
The GIS in the Company is implemented using internationally accepted data model
named ArcGIS Pipelines Data Model (APDM) that has been adapted to meet the
specific business needs. The APDM is a model written for liquid and gas
transmission pipeline operating companies interested in storing data in the ESRI
geodatabase. APDM is a template with a small set of required core feature classes
and attributes. The model is not intended to be a ‘standard’ but a template from
which operating companies can build a geodatabase model that meets the
business requirements of the company.

Since its creation in 2002, the Company’s Pipelines Division has been engaged in
an ambitious integrity management program on its assets which has generated
great amounts of data and information. After developing the central database, the
data of 48 pipelines were migrated into the central database in order to analyses
the data for decision making purposes and target a high performance integrity
management system.
There are three main types of classes in APDM Geodatabase:
• Feature Classes – Elements that have an associated graphic - any feature with
a geographic location. Feature Classes can optionally be grouped into a
Feature Dataset. A Feature Dataset contains a group of Feature Classes that
can logically be grouped.
o Polyline Feature Class
o Point Feature Class
o Polygon Feature Class
o Feature Dataset
• Object Classes (Events) – Elements that don’t have a graphic associated with
them – a table that contains information that isn’t tied to a specific
geographic location.
o Object Class (Table)
• Relationship Classes – Defines the relationship between Feature and Object
Classes.
o Relationship Class

B.13.14.2.2 Review of Standard
The specifications and guidelines provided as part of this Standard will be reviewed
and updated at the regular intervals.
B.13.14.3 Pipeline Construction related Geospatial Data Submission Requirements
The Company’s standard defines a Geospatial Data Submission (GDS)
specification for pipeline centerline related geospatial features and attributes. At the
core of the standard is a GDS template file for exchanging pipeline construction
related geospatial data as per the following procedure:
▪ The Company provides the Data Submitter with a template GDS file comprising
of the pipeline construction related geospatial data schema
▪ Data Submitter delivers the pipeline construction related geospatial data to the
Company as per the GDS template
The format and the content of the GDS template file are explained in the following
sections.
B.13.14.3.1 GDS File Format for Pipeline Construction Data
The format of the GDS file for pipeline centerline related geospatial features and
attributes shall be GIS format such as ESRI File Geodatabase. The GDS file
defines what coordinate system to use (including the UTM Zone no.), how map
features are organized into layers, how those layers are named, and how features
Page 146 of 214

INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY (IT)
ADNOC GAS EXHIBIT - B.13 REV. 1
in those layers are symbolized. The GDS file standard is intended solely to support
the needs of updating the Company’s central databases.
B.13.14.3.2 Template GDS File for Pipeline Construction Data
The data submitter shall follow the template provided by the Company. The
template is in ESRI File Geodatabase format. The geographical features such as
pipeline, valves, markers, and corridors just mention few present in the geospatial
data are linked to the tabular data of attributes using unique IDs present in both
geospatial data and attribute data.
As a minimum, the template GDS file shall contain the definition of the coordinate
system and all of the required geographic features as well as their attributes
predefined. Lookup values of some of the attributes are defined in order to
standardize the data collection and data standardization. However new attributes
can be added subject to Company approval. The template GDS file includes blank
tables to be used for the entry of attribute information. Some attributes contain list
of values with the appropriate abbreviations or terms to be entered / selected. The
data submitter shall provide the GIS data in the following Datum,
Native Coordinate System of GPS:
▪ Geographic Coordinates WGS84 datum
▪ Latitude/Longitude
▪ Horizontal Datum: WGS84
▪ Vertical Datum: RasGhumays
Nahrwan:
▪ Projected UTM Zones 39N and 40N
▪ Easting and Northing in Meters
▪ Horizontal Datum: Nahrwan 1967
▪ Vertical Datum: RasGhumays
It is strictly recommended to avoid pre-set GPS device with the Nahrwan coordinate
system. Contractor must provide GPS native files in the GPS native coordinate
system as defined above. The transformation from GPS native datum to Nahrwan
shall be performed through the GPS software after downloading native files from
the GPS to the computer. Contractor must provide the transformation parameters
used for transforming coordinates from GPS native datum to Nahrwan.
The UTM Zone numbers shall be included in the data as column/field; making it
clear which UTM Zone is to be used in the case of projects spanning the two UTM
Zones 39N and 40N.
Depending on the objective of the project, company may provide the contractor with
additional information as part of the template GDS file. This may include the project
area boundary, geodetic points, road networks, administrative boundaries,
topography, ortho-imagery, and geographic names to serve as reference data.
Now that the AD-SDI initiative is well under way, several FGDS data sets have
been established and are available on the AD-SDI geospatial portal. The portal
enables COMPANY to provide reference geospatial layers to the development,
surveying, and engineering communities.

The table below lists some of the required geographical entities from the GDS
template file, showing the layer name (Feature Class), topology, and the description
of the layer:
Layer Name Topology Description
Centerline – routing and stationing
Control Point Point Represent direction change. IP (Intersection Points). Stores
points of known x,y position and known station value (measure)
along the pipeline centerline. Control point features define the
pipeline centerline.
Depth of Point This table contains depth data for buried pipeline as well as
Cover water depth for sub-sea pipelines. For offshore pipelines, the
pressures in deep water will influence the risk assessment
results and this information is populated here.
Station Series Polyline Stores the routes that comprise the pipeline centerline, Each
vertex in the station series feature has a measure (or station)
value assigned to it.
Line Loop Table Collection of station series that comprise a single ‘line’.
Site Polygon Store the polygonal boundaries of the various stations and other
properties housing facilities owned by a pipeline company. Site
features might be used to define the boundaries of properties,
easements, temporary work areas, and large pipeline complexes
such as meter stations, compressor stations, refineries, custody
transfer stations, and valve stations. Site features may also be
used to demarcate the limit of stationed pipes and non-stationed
pipes.
External Doc Table Stores information describing the location and content of an
external file object stored on a disk drive, web folder or
document management system. The purpose of the External
Document object class is to link features and events with
external documentation using a similar method to that of the
ArcMap Hyperlink tool, but it stores the information within the
underlying object class so that external applications can access
the information. Assuming the appropriate application is installed
on the workstation, simply clicking on a properly populated File
Name or Hyper Link field in the ArcMap Identify tool window
causes the referenced document to open.
Facility – pipes, valves and other fittings
Pipe Polyline Used to model the primary conduit of pressurized product flow of
Segment a pipeline system: pipes. The typical length of pipe used in
transmission pipelines is 40 feet. Pipe segment features
aggregate many of these pipes into a single feature with
common attribute values.

Layer Name Topology Description
Casing Polyline Represents a protective structural device surrounding a pipe
segment. Casings are used to protect pipelines from the weight,
pressure, and vibration caused by traffic on roads, railroads, and
other types of line crossings.
Coating Polyline Represents the materials that are spread over a set of pipe
segments and fittings to preserve the metal from corrosion and
exposure to environmental conditions. Coating can be applied to
the internal and/or external surfaces of pipe segments.
Valve Point Contains information describing manufactured, pressurized
fittings used to control or impede flow of product through a
pipeline system. Valves provide the control structure for the
pipeline system and are often connected to the SCADA
monitoring system for a pipeline. Block Valve Stations & Isolation
Valve on Scrappers.
Appurtenance Point Store ad hoc, non-pressurized point features that are found on
and along a pipeline system. Pipeline Support & Protection (e.g
Slabs & Sleepers).
Tee Point Contains information describing manufactured branch or tee
fittings designed to carry pressurized product flow from a main to
a branch or secondary pipe. These facilities are used in the data
alignment module as well as the incorrect operations model of
the risk assessment module.
Insulated Point
Flange
Elbow Point Represents a bend in the pipeline at a specific angle. An elbow
is typically manufactured in angle increments of 15 degrees.
Vent Point Vent
Tap Point Stores information describing both manufactured tap fittings and
tap fabrication (hot taps) located on a pipeline system. The
APDM considers a tap to be the joining of two or more pipes at a
junction for the purpose of releasing product in a controlled
fashion. These facilities are used in the data alignment module
as well as the incorrect operations model of the risk assessment
module.
Vessel Point Describes large volume facility features that are used to contain,
process, or alter product on or along the pipeline.
Transition Point
Fitting
Pressure Test Polyline Store features describing pressure tests conducted along parts
of the pipeline.
Right Of Way Polyline Stores information describing easements and right-of-way
information of the pipeline as it passes through a property

Closure Point Closure feature class represents the terminus or endpoint of a
pipeline. A closure is designed to interrupt (and typically contain)
pressurized flow at the end of a pipe segment. Quick Closure Pig
Trap Door.
Sleeve Point Stores information about sleeves, clamps, reinforcements, and
other repair features that are applied around the girth of pipes.
Sleeve features do not typically overlap each other and are
dependent on the presence of a pipe segment feature.
Elevation Points Store elevations taken at specific points along the pipeline
Point centreline. Anytime that a section of pipe is excavated (or initially
placed in the ground) the depths of the pipeline features from the
ground surface are recorded.
Marker Point Stores information about monuments, aerial markers, mileposts,
and other offline features that determine position along a
pipeline.
Field Note Points Survey notes collected during preliminary routing of pipeline,
engineering, environmental, or cultural field surveys.
Encroachments – On the pipeline, within class corridor
Structure Or Polygon Stores information pertaining to any structure, identified site or
Site identified site area located within 660 feet of either side of the
pipeline centreline (class corridor). The Federal Department of
Transportation requires that pipeline companies maintain
information regarding any occupied structure within the class
corridor of the pipeline.
Nearest Point Point Contains the location of the nearest point on a structure to a line.
To Line
ID Site Area Polygon Store identified sites that are represented as polygons.
Structure Point Contains the online point locations for a Nearest Point To Line
Location feature that falls within a certain distance (usually 660 or 1,000
feet) of the centreline.
Structure Polygon Feature class contains the polygon outlines of structures.
Outline
Line Crossing Polyline Represents a set of linear features (roads, rivers, fences, etc.)
that intersect the centreline of the pipeline. Every pipeline
company must track any of these features for right-of-way

purposes, ownership purposes, and DOT/FERC safety
regulations.
Line Crossing Polyline Stores the online location of the easement to either side of a Line
Easement Crossing feature when it intersects the centreline.
Line Crossing Point Stores the online location where a Line Crossing feature
Location intersects the centreline.
Soil This table contains soil and land use information as it varies
along the distance of the pipeline. This information is used in the
condition assessment, risk assessment and ECDA modules.
Soil Data Line This table provides an indication to the system where solids, or
solid materials, are known to exist in the product as it travels
through the pipeline. This information is used in the risk
assessment module.
Cathodic protection
CP Test Point Stores the information describing a cathodic protection test
Station station.
CP Rectifier Point Stores information about a rectifier. A rectifier is a cathodic
protection device that manages the power conversion from AC
(alternating current) to DC (direct current) before it is passed on
to a pipeline.
CP Bond Point Stores information describing cathodic protection bonds that link
one or more bond wires together.
CP Cable Polyline Stores information about the cathodic protection cables that
carry current to/from different cathodic protection devices and
the pipe segments of the Pipeline
CP Anode Point Stores information about the anodes utilized in a pipeline
cathodic protection system.
CP Ground Point Location on or off the centreline where one or more anodes are
Bed placed.
CIS Point This table contains readings that were obtained from Close
Interval Survey (CIS) inspections when the data is stored as
point. Generally speaking, that data from a CIS is loaded into this
table.
DCVG Point This table contains readings that were obtained from Direct
Current Voltage Gradient (DCVG) inspections when the data is
stored as point.
CP Location Point Stores the online locations for CP Anode, CP Bond, CP Ground
Bed, CP Rectifier, and the CP Test Station offline point features
and the CP Cable offline polyline features.

Layer Name Topology Description
Operating Polyline Store features describing the current, designed, and maximum
Pressure allowable operating pressure zones along a pipeline.
Product Line This table contains the product chemistry as well as velocity and
Composition flow information for the pipelines in the system. As some of this
data can vary along the pipe (by way of other pipeline facilities
that may change velocity), the data is often defined in segments
that begin or end at the same location as other point data (such
as a reducer). The risk assessment module is heavily dependent
on the data in this table for some key attributes (H2S Content) as
well as presence or absence of the various density values for
single vs. multi-phase calculations.
Instruments
Instrument Points Stores information about facilities and equipment typically found
on a Process and Instrumentation Diagram (P&ID). Examples of
these types of equipment include pressure and temperature
sensors, pressure and temperature transmitters, pressure
regulators, level indicators, level controllers, valve position
indicators, valve positioners, gas samplers, gas chromatographs,
flow computers, E/R probes, etc.
Instrument Points Instrument feature may have multiple Instrument Parameter
Parameter records associated with it.
Meter Point Contains information describing manufactured, pressurized
fittings that are used to monitor flow, pressure, and composition
of product as it is transported along a pipeline.
Meter Point Meter Readings are measurements of monitored conditions
Reading provided by meter fittings which are taken at timed intervals and
retained for future use.
B.13.14.3.3 Submitted GDS File
The standard requires that the GDS file for pipeline construction related data be
submitted by the contractor to the Company and that it conforms to the format and
content requirements, as specified in the template GDS file provided to the data
submitter. The data submitter may add to the layers / attributes in the template file
subject to the company approval, but may not delete any of them.
B.13.14.3.4 Geospatial Data Submission
The contractor shall provide all the geospatial information acquired within the scope
of the project. These geospatial information include but not limited to geo-
referenced topographic maps, geo-referenced satellite images, ortho-rectified aerial
images, digital elevation model (DEM), digital terrain model (DTM), and

B.13.14.3.5 Documentation
Documentation accompanying the geospatial submittals as a printed document or,
preferably, as a plain text file on the same media as the geospatial submittals must
include:
▪ The name of the company and of the preparer of the GDS file
▪ The same project identifying information as on the delivery media
▪ The name and version of the software used to create the GDS file
▪ Project address or, if no address, a descriptive location of the project site
▪ Template Data Source (Originating Entity, and Project)
▪ The date or dates when the survey or engineering work was performed
▪ Data Acquisition Method
▪ A list of the file names on the delivery with a description of what each file
contains.

APPENDIX A: DATA DICTIONARY
The data dictionaries contain type of data collected from centreline surveys, geotechnical surveys,
environmental surveys, as-built surveys and other data collection activities. A well-planned data
dictionary that includes consistent field names, table structure, and content is crucial for data
integration purpose. By providing survey and environmental contractors with field data dictionaries
and the data collection training, the output files they provide are always consistent. The field data
dictionary is instrument independent, allowing for position data to be collected by DGPS. The
following few sections describe the centreline data dictionaries.
